* Queries 660 ("Wrecks here") and 661 ("Wreck hold") show the wrecks where the drone is and what is aboard one.

<a name="Kits"></a>**Two kits: miners and makers (#380).** The mining gear is big: a Worm (A1) and a Refinery (A1) take 120 cargo each, so a 500-cargo hull carried the mining set (Engine, Worm, Refinery: 296) or the factory set (Engine, Smelter, Chemical Reactor, Spider: 267), never both (563). Since migration 0029 (#380 round 37) hull 1 and the Cruisers carry 1,000, for fuel on long trips and bigger loads, so a drone that buys the other kit can do both; it still does one thing at a time. Refining has its own module, the Refinery (5221), instead of the Smelter. A release chooses its kit: Mining Kit I (5901, unpacked by 25901) or Factory Kit I (5902, by 25902). The House sells both kits for 2,000, and the Worm and Refinery for 1,000 each at Earth, so each kit's contents are worth 2,400. The House also sells ores and refined goods at every station (`HOUSE_MARKUP`: half as much again as it pays for them there; an ore at half as much again as what it refines to, less the fuel, so buying ore to refine never beats buying the refined goods). So a maker can always buy its inputs, but a miner who asks less sells first. The starters price their goods halfway between the House's two prices, and every design that buys compares a drone's sell order with the House (`buy-cheapest`). Before, every drone had every module, and round 24's makers mined their own metals: miners sold 12,341 Metals (A1) to the House and 966 to other drones.

<a name="Taxes"></a>**The sales tax (#380).** The seller pays `house_rules.sales_tax_permille` thousandths (50, so 5%) of every sale, to the nearest credit (`sales_tax` in `wreck.c`), out of what the buyer pays; the buyer pays the full price, and the tax vanishes (a sink). It applies to sales to other players (`event_do_market_transaction` in `event.c`; `trades.tax`) and to House stations (`st_clear_batch` in `db_station.c`; `station_offers.tax`); the House's own sales aren't taxed. Each tax paid is a `company_taxes` row. It replaced a daily tax (#324 step 12, #367): a share of a company's assessed wealth, goods included, every game day. Only credits paid that, so a trader holding stock could be eliminated with thousands of credits' worth aboard (round 24). The daily tax's columns (`tax_period`, now 0, `tax_grace`, `tax_flat`, `tax_permille`), the `company_taxable` view and the `founded_t`/`taxed_t` columns stay for old worlds, and the engine no longer reads them. A company eliminated by the daily tax kept `end_cause = tax`, and its drones `wreck_cause = tax`; the post-mortems still explain them.

**A company also ends when its last drone dies** (#324 step 13): whenever a drone becomes a wreck (`db_wreck_drone`, for any cause; a drone given up on after crashes goes through it too since #546, when it had its own update and its company traded on), a company left with no working drones ends the same way, with `end_cause = drones`: its credits vanish. A company eliminated by the old daily tax has `end_cause = tax`.

Query 650 ("My taxes") gives the sales tax rate and what the drone's company paid in the last game day and in all (both doubles). `test_wreck.c` checks the arithmetic of the tax and of plunder shares. The drone page's Activity shows each sale's tax.

<a name="Shares"></a>**Shares and dividends (#467, step 1 of the stock market #466).** Every company has 1,000 shares (`SHARES_PER_COMPANY` in `shares.h`; since #485 that's where it starts, see [New shares](#Share-offerings)), and each pays its holder 0.1% of the company's sales income after sales tax, rounded down (`dividend_for` in `shares.c`, checked by `test_shares.c`). Sales to House stations and drone-to-drone sales count alike. `shares` (migration 0038) holds who has how many of each company's shares, and the dividends each holder has had from it in all.
* A company starts holding all its own shares (the `companies_shares_insert` trigger, and the migration for those already there), so nothing changes until it parts with some.
* At every sale, after the tax, the seller's outside holders are paid (`db_dividends_due` and `db_pay_dividend` in `db_wreck.c`, called from `event_do_market_transaction` and from a station batch in `db_station.c`). A holder that is the buyer in the same sale is credited with its own balance; any other holder by `UPDATE companies`, with a credit event for one of its drones, as every other change of credits has. The remainder of the rounding stays with the seller. A company with no outside holders costs one index step a sale.
* A company that ends loses the shares it held, its own included: the `companies_shares_end` trigger gives them to the House (the lowest House company), which goes on being paid their dividends. By default the House will sell them once there is an exchange (#466 step 2).
* `php artisan forge:shares <company>` shows a company's holders and their dividends; `--to=<holder> --units=<n>` (and `--from=<holder>`) moves shares by hand (`App\Services\Shares`). Players can't trade shares yet: the Earth exchange, Lua functions, pages and the leaderboard's valuation of shares come in steps 2 and 3.
* Checked on the saved 1,000-ship world: a company with no drones given 100 shares of every player company was paid exactly 10% (rounded down per sale) of 789 sales' income after tax, 4,867 credits, and its balance was exactly its dividends.

<a name="Share-exchange"></a>**The share exchange at Earth (#469, step 2 of #466).** Shares trade the way goods do: standing orders, and drones answering them. Four protocols, each with three parameters and one Share event (type 14) that ends the action when the request reaches Earth, after the light time from the drone's place (`ProtocolInfo_t.share_delay`); each burns a unit of fuel:
* 41 posts a sell order (`perform(41, company, keep, price)`, Lua `sell_shares`), selling while the company holds more than `keep`; 42 posts a buy order (`perform(42, company, want, price)`, `buy_shares`), buying while it holds fewer than `want` and can pay. A company has one of each for each company's shares (`share_orders`, migration 0039): a newer one replaces it on arrival, a price of 0 withdraws it.
* 43 pulls shares from a sell order (`perform(43, n, company, seller)`, `pull_shares`) and 44 pushes them into a buy order (`perform(44, n, company, buyer)`, `push_shares`), at the order's price, all or nothing.
* The game engine checks the parameters (a price of at least 0, limits from 0 to 1,000, units of at least 1, companies still trading), records the request in `share_requests` and schedules the event. The events engine settles it (`db_shares_settle` in `db_shares.c`, from `event_apply`): it posts or withdraws the order, or checks a pull or push against the order now standing (`share_trade_check` in `shares.c`, checked by `test_shares.c`) and moves the shares and credits (`db_credit_company`, with credit events for both companies' drones) and records a `share_trades` row. A refusal is a failed event, and perform returns its outcome: -10 no such order (or the drone's own company's), -5 below the seller's kept minimum, -6 above the buyer's wanted maximum, -4 the buyer can't pay, -2 the seller doesn't hold them. No sales tax.
* Queries 690 and 691 (Lua `share_sell_orders`, `share_buy_orders`) list the orders for a company's shares that can trade now: sell orders whose poster holds more than it keeps, cheapest first; buy orders whose poster holds fewer than it wants and can pay for one, highest first.
* Once a game hour (`db_shares_clear_due`) the House posts sell orders, keeping none, for what it holds of other companies' shares (collected from failed companies) at `house_share_price`: 30 game days of dividends at the rate of the company's sales income after tax over the last game day (`trades`), at least 1. Ended companies' orders go.
* Checked in the saved 1,000-ship world with two released Lua companies: A posted a sell order keeping 900 at 5; B saw 100 on offer, pulled 60 (300 credits), and a second pull of 50 was refused with -5 (40 left above the 900); B posted a buy order wanting 100 at 4, A found it with `share_buy_orders` (B holding 60, room for 40) and pushed 30 (120 credits). A ended with 1,420 credits and 910 of its shares, B with 9,580 and 90.

<a name="Share-questions"></a>**Share questions, pages and wealth (#471, step 3 of #466).** Queries 692 (My shares: company, units, dividends, last trade price; Lua `shares_held()`), 693 (Shareholders of company N; `shareholders`) and 694 (company N's last 20 share trades with their game day; `share_trades`, and `share_price` from its newest). A company's last trade price is its newest `share_trades` row (`Shares::lastPrices`). `Shares::summary` gives the company page's Shares section and the API's `shares` field: the price, holders, standing orders, holdings in other companies with their value, and recent trades. The leaderboard's net wealth adds the shares a company holds in others at their last trade price.

<a name="Share-offerings"></a>**New shares to fund growth (#485, part of #475).** A company's shares are no longer fixed at 1,000: `companies.share_count` (migration 0041, default 1,000) is its count, and a share pays sales after tax × shares held ÷ the count (`dividend_for(net, units, total)` in `shares.c`; the House's share price divides by the count too). Shares in a young company sold for 1–2 credits in rounds 42–48 because investors pay for a slice of today's income; an offering with a target lets them pay for income after the growth it funds, since they pay only if the whole amount comes in. Two protocols, each with a Share event like 41–44 and a unit of fuel:
- **45 Offer new shares** `(M N P D)`: the drone's company offers up to N new shares, at least M (1 to N, #516), at P credits for D game days (1 to 30). `offer_check` refuses more than the company's count (at most doubling, -6), a second open offering (-9), or a minimum out of range. N 0 withdraws it, refunding everyone. The request rides in `share_requests` as kind 5, with D in `counterparty` and M in `share_company`; the offering in `share_offerings` (one a company; `min_units`, migration 0043, NULL for offerings posted before it: all of them). Lua's `offer_shares(n, price, days, min)` passes M = n when min is left out.
- **46 Commit to an offering** `(N C)`: the drone's company commits to N of company C's offered shares. `commit_check` refuses its own company's or no open offering (-10), more than is left (-6), or not enough credits (-4). The credits leave the investor at once (a credit event) and wait in `share_commitments`. The commitment that covers the last share fills the offering (`sh_fill_offering`): each investor's shares are created in `shares` and recorded as a `share_trades` row from the company at the offering price, the company is paid the whole amount, its count grows, and the offering goes.
- **Deadlines** (#516): the hourly housekeeping (`db_shares_clear_due`) closes offerings past their deadline. One with at least its minimum committed issues what was committed (`offer_issues` in `shares.c`, then `sh_fill_offering` with that many); one short of it, or of a company that ended, refunds every commitment (`sh_refund_offering`). It also drops commitments of companies that ended. Until #516 an offering was all or nothing: round 55 (#380) found one stuck at 277 of 278 shares for days, and investors' credits tied up in offerings that then lapsed. A minimum lets a company take what it can use, and the investors' credits come back into play sooner.
- There is no price floor (#485: by choice).
- **Dividends from profit (#603).** Until #603 `event.c` (drone-to-drone sales) and `db_station.c` (the old batch House sales) paid a sale's outside holders their part of it at once, after tax. Now `sh_settle_dividends` in `db_shares.c` runs in the hourly share housekeeping and settles each trading company once a game day for every complete day since `companies.dividend_day` (at most `DIVIDEND_LOG_DAYS`, 7, back, never before it was founded). `sh_day_figures` sums the day's trades of the company's drones: sales at their price less tax, purchases at their price, except that a trade between two companies with the same `user_id` counts at the market's price (`SH_TRADE_VALUE`: units × the resource's volume-weighted price over the 7 game days before the day's end in trades between different owners, else the trade's own price). Profit is sales − purchases; `dividend_pool(carry, profit)` in `shares.c` (tested in `test_shares.c`) makes up `companies.dividend_carry` (losses not yet made up, 0 or below) and returns what's left to share. `db_dividends_due` splits it by holding, as before, and the payouts are scaled down to the company's credits if they'd pass them. Each day is a `company_profits` row (migration 0048: sales, purchases, profit, carry_before, paid, holders, logged_only). A company never settled (every company when #603 went live) logs its last 7 days with `logged_only` = 1, paying nothing, so share prices have a profit history from the start. Queries 653, 654, 696 and 697 gained `profit` (`DAY_PROFIT`: the average of the days settled in the last 7), which Lua's `offering_cost`, `invest_surplus`, the investor and Maker examples and the House's asking price (`sh_house_orders`: 30 days of it) now use; `sales` is unchanged. The website's `Shares::dayProfits()` feeds the Stock exchange page's *Profit a day* and *A share a day*, and `CompanyEarnings` adds each day's `dividends`. Round 62 (#380): every buyer of shares earned 3 to 13 times what they paid, from dividends on gross sales.
- **A cap on outside ownership (#599).** Others may hold at most half a company: `SHARES_MAX_OUTSIDE_PERMILLE` (500) in `shares.h`, checked by `shares_over_cap(outside, count)` in `shares.c` (and `test_shares.c`). `sh_offer` refuses an offering that, fully taken, would pass it (`OFFER_OVER_CAP`, -6 like too many), and `sh_trade` refuses a company selling its own shares past it (`SHARE_OVER_CAP`). Holders trading among themselves, and companies already past it, are unaffected but for that. `Shares::MAX_OUTSIDE` mirrors the constant for the website, which shows how much others hold (`shares.outside`, `outside_share`, `max_outside` in the API) and warns from `WARN_OUTSIDE` (30%). Queries 653 and 654 gained `outside` and `history` (the game days of sales behind `sales`, 1 to 7), and Lua's `offering_cost` gained `outside_now`, `outside_after`, `over_cap`, `history` and `thin`. Round 62 (#380): Tobias 3's script crashed right after an offering, lost the global that remembered it, and offered on every trip (1,000 shares to 8,150); every seller priced on a day or two of sales.
- **Charts on the company page (#600).** `CompanyEarnings::daily()` (API `/companies/{id}/earnings`) sums a company's drones' trades by game day: sales after tax, purchases, net. `partials/earnings-chart` draws it (bars up for sales, down for purchases, a line for net). The share price chart is `partials/price-volume-chart` (#595) fed `/shares/{id}/history`, whose rows gained `average`, `trades`, `place` and `place_name` for the purpose; `partials/chart-styles` holds the look both charts share.
- Queries 697 (Share offerings; Lua `share_offerings()`: company, units, price, left, hours, count, sales, mine, min) and 698 (Share count; `share_count(c)`). The leaderboard counts committed credits as the investor's wealth, and the API's `shares.count` gives the count. Example designs: `raising_miner.lua` raises what its next copy still needs at most 2,500 at a time, taking half or more at the deadline (#516) and raising again for the rest, from its seventh day of income and never under 5 a share (#490) (open 10 days; #488, after round 49 found ten 4,000–5,000 raises competing for about 15,000 spare credits), and `investor.lua` and the Maker starter back one offering at a time, the one they've committed to until it fills or lapses, else the one closest to filling. The website (`Shares::offerings()`) shows open offerings on the Stock exchange page and the company's page, and the API's `shares.offering` gives a company's.

<a name="Share-valuation"></a>**Valuing shares (#492, #493, after round 53).** A company's sales come in lumps, so queries 696 and 697 (`DAY_SALES` in `build_game_data.py`), the House's share prices and the website (`Shares::daySales()`) take sales after tax over the last 7 game days, divided by 7 or by the company's age in days if younger (from `founded_t`). Lua `earnings_per_day(days)` gives a company its own. A company that has ended earns nothing, so its shares are worth nothing: they count 0 in net wealth (`Shares::holdingsValue()`), share books (690, 691), query 696 and the Stock exchange page leave them out, query 692 prices them at 0, the hourly housekeeping deletes the orders for them, the House doesn't list them, and a pull or push of them settles as no order (-10). Shares of a live company count at their last trade price: net wealth is a company's current value, what its holdings sell for now, not dividends it might earn later (#493, by choice). Since #513 (rounds 55–57) that current value is the average of the last 3 game days' share trades, weighted by units, falling back to the last trade (`Shares::valuePrices`; query 692's `price`, `VALUE_SHARE_PRICE`; the API's `value_price`; a Value column on the Stock exchange page). The last trade alone could be pushed up by two shares traded between a player's own companies (round 55: Wren 2's holders jumped from 3–6 to 18) or knocked to 1 by one panic sale (round 57: Dev 6 from 5 to 1), and every holder's net wealth moved with it.

<a name="Example-safety"></a>**Safer examples (#496, after round 53).** Every starter and company design calls `fuel_floor(15)` first thing in `launched`, so making, mining, refining and pushing stop with enough fuel left to buy more: five newcomer companies in round 53 died of fuel, one of them built on the Foundry example. `investor.lua` keeps `fuel_money` (200) whatever its `cash_floor`, never offers shares under what it paid (`paid_for`, counted when it asks), and calls its status function `show_status` instead of replacing the game's `report`. The miners say why they leave the Moon in their status, and `dry_tries` notes that a large value keeps them there.

<a name="Fuel-tracing"></a>**Tracing fuel (#494, #499, after round 53).** Five newcomer companies in round 53 died of fuel and nobody could say where it went. Six of round 53's nine fuel wrecks were a bug (#499). `db_refund_fuel` (the fuel a failed pull or push gives back, round 38) read the drone's fuel from its newest event naming fuel, of any type. A wait (type 12) or a share request (type 14) names fuel with 0, so after one the refund set the tank to the refund alone (86 became 1), and the next power bill wrecked the drone. It now reads the fuel from `holdings` and the cargo from the newest inventory event. To make the next one traceable: the drone page and API's `fuel_last_day.by_action` break the day's fuel down by action; `GET /api/v1/drones/{id}/timeline` pages through every action (`?before=`, `?limit=` up to 100); the post-mortem shows 25 entries and the wreck's last trades; and the API's `trades` are a wreck's too (they were empty).

<a name="Starting-credits"></a>**Credits instead of a kit (#506, answering #504).** A released company starts at Earth with 100 fuel and 3,000 credits (`forge.starting_credits`: the 1,000 it had plus the 2,000 a kit costs) and no kit; `Release::release` ignores the `kit` argument and leaves `companies.start_kit` null. A player who didn't want the kit lost about 1,000 net wealth selling it (Earth paid about 950 for what net wealth counted at 2,000), so a pure investor started behind. Scripts equip themselves: Lua `equip(kit)` unpacks a kit aboard, buys `kit` with `buy_here` if the drone was released (`lineage().parent` 0) or waits for its parent's if it is a clone. The starters and designs call it (the trader buys only an Engine, the investor nothing). Net wealth subtracts the starting credits and fuel (`Leaderboard::kitValue`). The Earth Exchange sells 400 kits of each kind a day (100 in stock) and 100 of each module, so a crowd of releases can equip at once.

<a name="Round-55-fixes"></a>**Round 55 fixes (#423, #424, #425, #503, #509, #511, #512, #533).**
- **Library.** `crowd`, `sold` and `board_price` ask about here when the place is left out (#423: `crowd()` crashed). `buy_here(n, r, max_price)` buys only from sellers asking at most `max_price` (#503); with #521 it reads the book before each pull and skips sellers that refused, which also answers #425.
- **`best_sites` (query 410, #424)** listed every place a resource can be mined, about 177 for yalspar, all at abundance 5. It now returns the 10 best: richest, then most left to mine now (the deposit's lazy refill, as query 640), then nearest to the drone (`objects` coordinates), with `units` and `distance` (thousand km).
- **Examples.** The investor and the Maker never skip the commitment that completes an offering (#509). The Miner, Refiner and Maker starters take `grow_until`, a game day after which they launch no copies (0, the default, never stops: the live game has no end; #512). The Foundry sells everything aboard when credits fall below its cash floor, before buying fuel (#512). The Maker's sell check compares with Earth's best bid from `quotes` as well as the board (#503). The Moon page shows `deposit(mineral)`, not FORTH.
- **The Miner and a crowded Moon (#533).** The drone a player releases starts at the quieter of Phobos and Deimos when more than `crowd_limit` (30) drones are at the Moon; round 55 had 98 there on day 1.
- **Fuel on failed purchases (#511).** Not a charge: every one of round 55's 30,146 failed pulls had its fuel given back (`db_refund_fuel`). Xan 5's last 4 fuel went on its power bill (protocol 2) while it retried a fuel purchase with 3 credits for half a day. The players' manual now says to keep credits for fuel and to sell or wait when broke.


<a name="Round-56-asks"></a>**Where to trade, and company health (#536, #537: round 56's top asks).**
- `where_to_buy(r, n, hour_value)` and `where_to_sell(r, n, hour_value)` (Lua library) group `orders_everywhere(r)` (query 615, an hour old elsewhere) by place, walk each place's orders best first for n, and add the round trip: `trip_to(place).hours` × 2 at `hour_value` (10) a game hour, and 10 fuel (travel, protocol 5001, burns 5) at the cheapest fuel price here. Places that can't fill n are left out. Abel's top change: metals ran out at Earth and the Moon from day 28 to 40 while Phobos sold them at 8.
- Company health: `App\Services\CompanyHealth` and query 699 (`company_health(c)`) share one SQL (`CompanyHealth::sql`, `company_health_sql` in `build_game_data.py`): working and wrecked drones; per working drone its free fuel (`holdings`) and, from its fuel events over the last game day (`events_inventory_time`, `LAG` over the drone's fuel), what it used (the falls) and its net fall; `days` is the least of fuel ÷ net fall over the drones whose fuel fell (-1 if none). `at_risk`: no working drone, or `days` ≤ 3 with credits under 500. The API's `/companies/{id}` has `health`, and the company and Stock exchange pages show it. Zane's top change: three of the companies Zane held ran dry, and their shares went to 0 unannounced. About 20 ms a company on round 56's world.

<a name="Round-57-fixes"></a>**Round 57 fixes (#513, #540, #541, #542).**
- **Share value (#513):** net wealth, query 692's `price` (`shares_held()`), the company page and the API's `value_price` value a share at the last 3 game days' share trades averaged by units, else the last trade (`Shares::valuePrices`, `VALUE_SHARE_PRICE`). Cora's top change.
- **Round 70 (#715):** a new company has `STALLED_GRACE_DAYS` (7) game days to make its first sale before "it has never sold anything" stalls it; half its drones idle stalls it only when it hasn't sold for a game day either (a company paying dividends was "stalled"); and `failing` lists the actions its working drones tried and failed over the last game day (`actions.outcome` < 0 but not -9, power bills left out, found through `events_drone_game_t`), by protocol, with `failing_reason` in words, and each of `drones` has `failing` too. A loop failing every few hours read as idle. The company page shows a **Failing** line, the Stock exchange a **failing** state.
- **Stalled (#540):** `CompanyHealth` and query 699 add `idle`, the game hours since any working drone's last inventory event for a resource other than fuel (types 1 and 2, `events_drone_game_t`, back at most 10 game days; `CompanyHealth::lastWorkSql`). `stalled` is idle ≥ 24. Since #689 the site and API also call a company stalled when at least half its working drones are idle a game day (`idle_drones`), or when it has sold nothing for `STALLED_SALE_HOURS` (72) game hours once it is that old (`last_sale`, from `trades` by seller); `stalled_reason` says which, and the company page shows a banner at the top. Query 699 (Lua's `company_health`) keeps the narrow rule. `CompanyHealth::drones` lists each working drone's idle hours, `script_status` and hold for the API's `health.drones` and the company page. Dev's and Ezra's top change.
- **At-risk reason (#541):** `at_risk_reason` says which test flagged a company. The manual's example sells at half the value price, not 1: round 57's investors dumped at 1 and every holder was marked down.
- **Examples (#542):** the Miner, Refiner and Maker launch a copy only above `grow_line()`: 5,000 plus `work_cash` (1,500) for each working drone after the launch (`company_health().working + 1`). They call `raise_cash(150)` before buying fuel. Lua `raise_cash(n)` sells goods aboard (resources under 5000, not fuel) into the best bids here, lifting the library's fuel floor for it, until the company has n credits. The Maker hauls its rods to `where_to_sell`'s best place (Earth unless another pays more after the trip).

<a name="Round-58-fixes"></a>**Round 58 fixes (#550, #551).**
- **House demand (#550, Hana's one change):** `HouseMarket::demand($resource, $place, $company)` and query 706 report the station drone's live buy order for each good it buys, as the order book counts it (`ApiController::movable`): `price` is its bid and `left` the units it can still take (#608; they worked this out from `house_market` and the station's holdings until round 63, when the two drifted apart and newcomers flew goods to bids that weren't on the book). `wanted()` posts a day's demand above the stock kept to sell, the bid falling to half the listed price at a full day bought. Past a full day bought (#691: `station.lua` ships down once a game day, after a long round of restocking, so a station can hold several days' worth, and Mars's Metals (A2) showed left 0 for a day), it posts the holding plus a quarter of a day's demand at `HouseMarket::bidFloor` (half the listed price, #739; until round 71 `listed × daily ÷ (2 × bought)`, at least 1, and Mars bid 2 to 5 for metals, Earth 67 for rods listed at 181, since a station's own unsold stock counts as bought). The holding counts units on their way in (`HouseMarket::incoming`, its pending increments, as `movable` does), or `left` read 0 while a round of imports landed. Every good it buys, so an overstocked exchange always bids, never under half. `sold` and `mine` count today's trades into it (all, and the asking company's), and `empty_hours` is `left` at today's pace; `hours` runs to the start of the next game day, when `station.lua` ships what it bought down (`ship()`). Lua `house_demand(r)`; `/api/v1/house-demand?resource=&place=`; the Market page's House demand table (one good everywhere with `?house=`, else the 30 bids closest to full). Keep the PHP, the query and `wanted()` in step.
- **House supply (#630, Bram's one change in round 64):** `HouseMarket::supply($resource, $place)` reports each station drone's live sell order for each good it sells (`side` 2), as the order book counts it (`on_sale` = movable less the quantity kept), `refill_to` (`station.lua`'s `stock()`: a quarter of the day's units), today's sales, and from `restocks()` the station's Import Resource events (protocol 5911) over two game days: the last import of each good, and its rounds, imports more than `ROUND_GAP` (30 game minutes) apart starting a new one; `round_hours` is the median gap between round starts (three rounds at least) and `next_hours` the last start plus that, less now. The website tick stores it at most every `SUPPLY_EVERY` (10) real seconds, about 5 game hours (`storeSupply`, table `house_supply`, migration 0053; until #710 once a real minute, which at the normal pace is more than a game day, so drones read a day-old table) for query 707, `house_supply(r)`, whose times are game minutes. `/api/v1/house-supply?resource=&place=`; the Market page's House supply table (one good with `?supply=`, else the 30 goods closest to sold out).
- **Shares API (#551, Gus's one change):** `/api/v1/shares` returns `Shares::exchange()` (the Stock exchange page's data); `/api/v1/shares/{id}/history` returns `Shares::history()`, a company's share trades by game day (low, high, last, units).
- **invest_surplus (#551):** Lua `invest_surplus(keep, days, cap)` ranks open offerings (a share's income spread over the shares once the offering fills) and listed shares (any seller but your company) by days of a share's income to pay back, and commits to or pulls the best within `days` (40), skipping buys under 25 credits unless one completes an offering. The Foundry, Systems and Electronics designs call it once a game day with a keep of 9,000 (8,500 for Electronics) while they can still grow, 3,000 after. Round 58: every investor was out of cash by about day 10 and offerings sat unbacked, while producers sat on 11,000 credits.

<a name="Code-pushes"></a>**Code pushes (#553).** A company released with a password (the Release page's optional field, the API's `password`; at least 6 characters) can push a new script to its drones (`App\Services\CodePush`). Round 58's Fay asked for it (7.5 to 9): every fix meant a new company from day 0, so late learning counted for nothing.
- **The password** is kept only as a bcrypt hash (`companies.push_password`; `launch_queue.push_password` for a queued release, set on the company when it launches) and checked with `Hash::check`. One password can serve all a player's companies; little is at stake if it leaks. A company released without one can't be pushed to.
- **The fee** is `forge.push_fee_permille` (50) × the push's number thousandths of the company's credits, at most `forge.push_fee_cap_permille` (500): 5%, 10%, 15%... of what it has, nothing at 0 or below. A young or failing company is cheap to fix and tinkering gets dear. The debit is conditional on `pushes` being unchanged, so two pushes at once can't both pay the first rate. Each push is a `code_pushes` row (number, game time, credits before, permille, fee, drones, script), shown on the company page and in the API's `pushes`.
- **Delivery** reuses release commands: each working Lua drone (not a wreck, not a station) gets `<reset>`, the script's pieces (`Release::chunks`) and `<launched>`, subject `Push`. The engine stamps them like any player's commands, so they arrive at the speed of light from Earth (`engine_stamp_commands`). `<reset>` is a meta command, so it runs although the old script still has a resume queued; it drops the Lua drone, clears `lua_script` and `lua_state` and deletes its resumes, so the pieces load and `<launched>` builds the new program. The drone keeps its hold, place and equipment; an action under way finishes in the events engine. Variables start fresh: a copy tells itself apart with `lineage()`.
- **Checked** by `CodePushTest` and the smoke test ("Smoke Pusher": a wrong password is refused, the right one switches the drone's `set_status` from v1 to v2 and charges 5%).

<a name="Company-age"></a>**Company age in Lua (#549).** Query 652 and `company_age(c)` give whole game days since a company's `founded_t` (#548), yours if c is left out. The Foundry, Systems and Electronics designs grew only while `today()` (the world's day) was 24 or less, so a company released on day 8 could grow for 16 days and one released after day 24 never did (round 58: a Foundry sat on 11,000 credits). They now use `company_age()`, as does their `invest_surplus` keep. The starters' `grow_until` stays on the world's day: it is a contest deadline ("set this about 20 days before the end"), not an age.

<a name="Round-59-fixes"></a>**Round 59 fixes (#556, #557, #558).**
- **equip (#556, Kira's one change):** `equip(kit)` loops while any of that kit's tools is missing (`kit_tools`: Mining Kit 5001, 5131, 5221; Factory Kit 5001, 5201, 5211, 5501), not while the Engine is. It unpacks that kit if it is aboard, waits if it is a new copy without an Engine, else buys one here (printing why once) or flies to Earth. Before, a miner's `equip(factory_kit)` returned at once, and a pushed script (#553) that wanted other tools got none. Checked in a test world: a drone with a Mining Kit's tools bought and unpacked a Factory Kit.
- **Script length (#557):** `/check` takes any length and reports `length` and `max_length` (`forge.max_script_length`, 16,000), refusing over it and warning within a tenth of it; the manual and `api.md` state the limit.
- **Push preview (#558, Iris's one change):** `/check` with `company` adds `push` (`CodePush::preview`: whether it takes pushes, the push's number, credits, fee percent and fee now, working drones), charging and sending nothing.

<a name="Share-claims"></a>**Shares others hold count against the company (#568).** Round 59's Jonah 3 reached #1 overall on day 14 mostly on credits from selling about 72% of its future income: net wealth counted the credits a sale brought in but nothing for the income given away. `Leaderboard::wealth` now subtracts `Shares::claimsValue()`, the shares of each company held by others (all holders but itself) at its value price (`Shares::valuePrices`, #513), the same price `holdingsValue` counts them at for the holders. Across all companies, shares net to zero; a sale at the value price leaves both sides' net wealth unchanged; a sale above it gains the seller, below it loses. Offering fills are share trades too, so they set the value price. Jonah's one change (7.5 to 9).

<a name="Round-60-fixes"></a>**Round 60 fixes (#570, #571, #572, #573, #574).** The first round on the live site.
- **Standing orders (#571, Nora's one change):** orders belong to the drone and outlive a push. Query 656 and Lua `standing_orders()` list a drone's orders that can still trade (from `latest_orders`: a sell order keeping under 1,000,000, a buy order wanting any); `cancel_orders()` replaces each with one that can't (`sell_order(price, 1000000, r)`, `buy_order(price, 0, r)`), since orders can't be deleted. `CodePush::preview` lists each drone's (`orders`, `orders_note`).
- **Offering cost (#572, Lena's one change):** Lua `offering_cost(n, price)` from `company_figures()`: `share = n / (count + n)`, `a_day = sales × share`, `payback_days = n × price / a_day`, `value_now = n × value`. `Shares::offerings()` adds `income_percent` and `dividends_a_day`, shown on the Stock exchange page and the company page.
- **Value price (#573):** `Shares::valuePrices()` and `VALUE_SHARE_PRICE` (query 692, 653, 654) average a company's share trades by units, newest first: every trade of the last 3 game days, and older ones while the newer don't cover half the shares others hold (`SUM(units) OVER (ORDER BY id DESC)`); else the last trade. Round 60's single trade of 109 shares at 24 tripled Lena 2's value and (#568) cut its net wealth by 9,000 in a day; with this rule its value is about 10. The two must stay in step.
- **Companies (#574, Milo's one change):** query 653 and Lua `companies()`: every company still trading but the House, best sales first, 100 at most: sales (`DAY_SALES`), share count, value, age, working drones. Query 654 and `company_figures(c)`: one company's.
- **equip (#570):** a copy (`lineage().parent` > 0) missing the kit's tools always waits for its parent's kit and never buys one; the message names the missing tool.

<a name="Processor-tax"></a>**Processor tax instead of thinking fuel (#544).** From PF step 8 (#340) a drone's thinking burned 1 fuel per `POWER_INSTRUCTIONS` instructions (200,000 since #380 round 37), and a drone whose tank ran dry was a wreck. Rounds 55–57 lost drones that way: Xan 5 retried a fuel purchase while broke, three of Yara's companies re-planned every 90 minutes, and Cora 7 ran a query inside a `table.sort` comparator (470 fuel). The requester: "It may be better to not burn fuel but possibly return to assessing a tax for total company processor time. That will make drones more resilient. It will also encourage coders to be more conservative in the number of calculations they run since that ultimately impacts real life server performance and the size of the game."
- **No fuel for thinking.** `POWER_INSTRUCTIONS` is "0" in `game-engine.conf`. `db_take_power_due` still squares a loading drone's `power_paid` (loading a released script stays free), then returns without billing.
- **The bill.** Once a game hour, in its own transaction, the events engine (`db_compute_bill_due` in `db_compute.c`, after the share exchange's housekeeping) reads each working drone's unbilled instructions (`agents.instructions - power_paid`). Loading drones (`release_vm IS NULL`) and the House are left out. `compute_credits_due` (in `compute.c`, checked by `test_compute.c`) turns them into whole credits at `house_rules.compute_tax_per_million` (15, about what the fuel cost: 5 fuel a million at about 3 credits). The bill moves `power_paid` on by the instructions those credits cover, so the rest carries over. Each company pays its debt and the new bill from its credits, as far as they go (`compute_pay`, `db_credit_company` with a credit event); the rest is `companies.compute_debt` (migration 0044). Each bill is a `compute_bills` row; `compute_clock` holds the last billed hour. A rate of 0 makes thinking free.
- **Throttled while it owes.** The turn row (`db_turn_row_load`) carries whether the drone's company owes (`throttled`). When such a drone's slice runs out, the engine queues its resume `COMPUTE_THROTTLE_SECONDS` (a game hour) later (`db_delay_resume_commands`). Its waits and actions are as before; only its thinking slows. Shortening the slice did nothing on a quiet server: the engine just ran ten times as many turns. In a test world a drone thinking about 6 million instructions a game hour paid hourly bills until its 3,000 credits ran out (about 3 game days). Then it ran about 1.6 million instructions every 30 real seconds instead of 36 to 40 million. Its debt grew slowly, it burned no fuel, and it was not wrecked.
- **The congestion tax (#620).** After round 64 the clock slowed to about 85× of its 1,800× target: 695 working drones on one game engine, about 300 of them round 64's pump-making companies breeding after the round (Aiko 3 alone ran 109). A flat rate let a growing fleet slow everyone without feeling it. Of more shards, a fair share of turns, a congestion tax and retiring the test fleets, the project lead chose the tax. Each hourly bill reads `game_clock.scale` and `target_scale`: the congestion is target ÷ scale, from 1 to `house_rules.congestion_cap` (10; 1 or less turns it off; migration 0051). A flat 10 times would have cost a newcomer's lone drone (0.3 million instructions a day, about 5 credits) 45 a day against a miner's 50 of income, so each company pays its share of it: `compute_company_congestion` = 1 + (congestion − 1) × its instructions billed over the last game day ÷ `congestion_full_at` (5 million; at most 1; 0: everyone pays all of it). Its drones are billed at `compute_congested_rate(base, that)`, rounded. The bill records the busy-company rate and the congestion in `compute_clock.rate_now` and `congestion`; the website (`ProcessorTax::congestion`, `companyCongestion`, `of`), the API (`compute`, `/clock`'s `processor_tax`) and query 651 work out each company's rate the same way. A rising rate makes busy fleets shrink or think less until the clock recovers: a carrying capacity for the world.
- **Seen.** Lua `compute_tax()` (query 651: the rate billed now, tax and instructions over the last game day, debt); the API's `compute` (`ProcessorTax::of`: `instructions_day`, `tax_day`, `debt`, `throttled`, `per_million`); the company page; net wealth subtracts the debt (`Leaderboard::wealth`, `ProcessorTax::debts`). The drone page's fuel line no longer shows thinking (except for a drone that burned fuel for it before #544), and a fuel post-mortem mentions computing only for such drones.

<a name="Winding-up"></a>**A matured company that sells nothing for 60 game days is wound up (#875).** Round 77 found matured companies holding 300,000 to 735,000 credits whose locked code had stopped selling 240 game days before; nobody could reach the credits, and their idle fleets crowded the mining sites. `WindUp::runIfDue()`, on the scheduler's tick at most every 5 real minutes, ends each matured company (not the House's, not a startup) whose last sale, or its maturing if it has sold nothing since, is `WindUp::DAYS` (60) game days old: its credits less processor tax owed are divided by its share count (the bank's shares included, and the bank's part stays with the bank), each player's shares are paid into their credits, a sale to the bank still unpaid is paid at no more than that, its share orders go, its drones leave the game with what they hold (`wreck_cause` `wound_up`, plundered at once), and the company ends (`end_cause` `wound_up`); `company_windups` keeps what each share was paid. From `WindUp::WARN` (30) game days without a sale, the company page, the stock exchange's health column and its owner's strip count the days down, and the "at risk or stalled" e-mail notice covers it.

<a name="Deposits-never-dry"></a>**Mining sites never run dry (#866).** With `house_rules.deposits_deplete` off (the default since migration 0089), `db_take_deposit` gives a mining run all its abundance gives, however many drones mine there, and keeps no `deposit_state`, so every deposit reads full (queries 640 and 410, `deposit()`, `deposits()`). Round 77's new Miners waited at dry sites for most of their first 40 game days while about 350 drones, mostly matured companies' fleets, worked the Moon, Phobos and Deimos. Turning the rule on brings back the deposits of #324 step 11 (400 runs' worth, refilling 80 a game day); the docker smoke test turns it on to check them. Every site yields the same (abundance 5), so the Miner and Refiner starters now keep themselves and their copies at the Moon, nearest the markets (`crowd_limit` 0, `spread_copies` 0).

<a name="Stalls"></a>**Downtime doesn't count (#837).** On 2 May 2038 every small matured starter company dissolved in the same minute: the world had stood still for about 100 game days (no drone acted), but game time follows the wall clock, so the dissolve timer kept counting and the next bill after the stall ended them all. Each hourly processor-tax bill (`db_compute_bill_due`) now notes the newest event id (`compute_clock.last_event_id`, migration 0091); when none is newer at the next bill, no drone acted that hour (the House's stations act whenever turns run), so every broke company's `broke_since` moves forward by the hour, and the stretch is kept in `world_stalls`. The wind-up (`WindUp::idleDays`) leaves those stretches out of a company's days without a sale. The tax itself is billed as before.

<a name="Dissolving"></a>**A company broke for 50 game days dissolves (#547).** The requester: "Rather than wrecking a drone that has no fuel lets dissolve a company that has zero or negative credits for 50 game days straight. When a company dissolves all its drones become salvageable wrecks." Since #544 a drone that runs dry is stranded, not wrecked.
- **Broke** means `credits - compute_debt <= 0`. Once a game hour, after the processor tax bill, `db_compute_bill_due` sets `companies.broke_since` (migration 0045) for a newly broke company and clears it for one that isn't broke any more.
- A company broke since at least `house_rules.dissolve_days` (50; 0: never) game days ago **dissolves**:
  - its working drones become wrecks (`wreck_cause = 'dissolved'`), holds and parts aboard, to plunder;
  - the company ends (`end_cause = 'dissolved'`, credits 0), so its shares are worth 0 (#493).
- The House is never dissolved.
- **Seen:**
  - `ProcessorTax::broke` (`broke_days`, `dissolves_in`) in the API's `compute` and in health;
  - `at_risk_reason`, which counts down;
  - the company page;
  - Lua `compute_tax().broke_days` (query 651);
  - the post-mortem.
- **Test world** (`dissolve_days` set to 1): a company with 0 credits dissolved within about a game day and its drone became a wreck; a company with 3,000 was untouched.

<a name="Share-designs"></a>**Investor designs (#473, step 4 of #466).** (The investor, listed-miner and raising-miner example designs were removed in #538: under the new rules companies can't trade shares.) Query 696 (Shares for sale; Lua `shares_for_sale()`) lists every sell order for shares that can sell now, cheapest first (50 at most), with the company's sales income after tax over the last game day, so a script can estimate a share's dividends (0.1% of it a day). It sums the company's drones' trades through a new index on `trades` (seller, game_t, migration 0040), which the House's share prices use too. Example designs: `investor.lua` (trades shares only, from Earth) and `listed_miner.lua` (the miner starter, offering part of its company). Since #475 step 2 the listed miner prices its shares from its income alone (no sales, no offer: price 0 withdraws it) and, once it has sold some, launches copies at 6,500 credits instead of 7,000, so what it raises goes into drones. Since #475 step 3 the investor looks further ahead: once a game day it learns what a share of each company pays a day (from the dividends it has had where it holds shares, else from the company's sales), smoothed over about a week. It pays a company up to 30 days of that, up to 60 for one whose income is growing, but pays another holder no more than 30 days, so investors don't bid shares up between themselves. It offers what it holds at 60 days, re-posting an order only when its price changes, and skips buys worth less than 25 credits, since every share request burns a unit of fuel (#481). It doesn't estimate growth from `share_trades`: in rounds 42 and 43 the trade prices mostly echoed the investors' own. Since #479 the listed miner asks 30 days of a share's income (about what investors pay), averaged over its last 7 game days, from its third day of income and at most 50 more shares a day, so a slow day doesn't give the company away. From #482 until #853 the Maker starter invested: once a game day it put credits above `invest_above` (3,000), up to `invest_cap` (1,500) in all, into shares companies sold of themselves at up to 30 days of a share's income (removed in #853: under the new rules companies can't trade shares); `words.lua` had the same as `invest_spare(above, days)`. In round 47 trading stopped once the six investors' 6,000 credits were spent, while Makers held about 3,700 each. The website's Stock exchange page (`/market/shares`, #484; `Shares::exchange()`) lists every company whose shares have traded, are on offer or are held by others, with its last price, last day's sales after tax, a share's income a day and the days its last price pays back; the sell orders that can sell now (as query 696), the buy orders still buying, and the last 25 trades. `forge:load-test --designs` releases a design with the kit its header names ("Release it with the Mining Kit"), else the factory kit. Round 42 in the economy playbook has the playtest.

**Event order.** The Game Engine reads the game clock once per loop, so on a fast clock the events engine can be ahead of it. An event stamped earlier than one already processed would be worked out from older state and undo it. Also, `sqInventory` takes a drone's highest event ID as its current state, so IDs must keep game-time order. So `protocol_flush_events` reloads the clock and starts each new action a game second after the drones' latest event, done or still to come (`db_get_latest_processed_game_t`). A script waits for its own action anyway, so in practice this mostly delays a power bill until the current action ends.

When the GE runs a `perform`, it computes the times and resource requirements of the in-game activity (change orbits, mine ores, transfer cargo, etc.) and logs the events that will happen, which the Event Engine processes when they are due.

*Planned, not built:* a "passive" state in which a script pauses (via a CatchEvent word) until the drone observes a particular event.

[Return to the TOC](#Table-of-Contents)

### Equipment
The following types of modules can be added to drone hulls and used to perform various activities.  Each of class of module consists of variants which perform differently.
* Power plants generate the energy used by the ship’s modules.
* Fuel tanks contain the reactants that are consumed by power plants.
* Propellent tanks store the gases that are energized and expelled by the engines to accelerate the drone.
* Engines energize and release the propellant to accelerate the ship.
* Fuel scoops skim the atmospheres of planets to fill the fuel and propellant tanks.
* Transfer bots shuttle cargo between adjacent drones.
* Mining bots are used to extract ores from asteroids and comets.
* Refineries process ores in to minerals.
* Forges turn raw materials into functional parts by following blueprint instructions.
* Construction bots are required to install modules in drone hulls.

## Players and Resources

### Player and Drone Tables
The following tables are used to record basic data about the players, their drones, and the onboard resources.

#### Table: users
The Users Table will hold information specific to each player account.
* id [INT(5)] -  This is the table index. Max 65,534 (FFFF).
* email [VARCHAR(255)] - A player account's e-mail address (#636), in lower case: one account per address, private to the account, used only to verify it. NULL for pupils' accounts in a class group.
* name [VARCHAR(45)] - This is the in-game name that is used by the player.
* password [VARCHAR(255)] - This is the player’s password.
* created_at [TIMESTAMP] - The date that the use first created their account.
* updated_at [TIMESTAMP] - The date that the record was last updated.
* credits [INT(10)] - Until the new rules (#635), unused: bank accounts belong to companies (below). Under the new rules, a player account's credits: what its shares and companies have earned it.
* login [VARCHAR(32)] - A player account's name (#636), unique without regard to case; it logs in with this, not its e-mail address. NULL for the game's own owners (the Public Forge owner, the House), which can't log in.
* share_credits [INT] - A player account's starting credits that can only buy shares (#636: `forge.player_share_credits`, 5,000), given when its e-mail address is verified.
* class_group_id [INT] - The class group a pupil's account belongs to (#636), or NULL.
* api_token [CHAR(64)] - The SHA-256 of the account's API token (#636). A new token replaces the old.
* email_verified_at [TIMESTAMP] - When the account's e-mail address was verified (#636), or NULL.

Player accounts (#636) are step 1 of the new rules (#635), and exist only while `FORGE_NEW_RULES` is on (it is on live since the reset, #642; with it off, the pages and API calls below answer 404). The rules themselves are in [The Rules of Play](#The-Rules-of-Play). An account is a name, a password and an e-mail address, one account per address (kept in lower case, private to its account); one IP address may make `forge.player_signups_per_hour` (3) accounts an hour. A link sent to the address (signed, good for 2 real days, `/account/verify/{id}/{hash}`) verifies it, and the first time brings the account its `forge.player_share_credits`; it can be sent again 3 times an hour. Pupils' accounts in a class group have no address (`email` NULL), count as verified through their teacher's, and get their share credits when made; only a verified account can make a class group. The website has `/signup`, `/login`, `/logout` and `/account` (credits, credits for shares, the address and whether it is verified, a new API token); the API has `POST /api/v1/accounts` and `POST /api/v1/login` (each returns a token), `GET /api/v1/account` (with `Authorization: Bearer <token>`) and `POST /api/v1/account/verify`. `App\Services\Players` does the work; mail goes through the `MAIL_*` settings (hosting.md).

#### Startups (#637)
Step 2 of the new rules (#635), only while `FORGE_NEW_RULES` is on. Every release is a **startup**, the logged-in player's (until #871 also a guest's, with a claim code shown once, which a player claims it into their account with, `guest_key` keeping its SHA-256). The bank lends it its hull (`forge.startup_hull_value`, 1,000: about what the House pays for a kit) and its starting credits (`forge.starting_credits`, 3,000), plus a fee (`forge.startup_fee`, 200), and it owes all three (`companies.debt`, 4,200). #658 cut it from 5,500, the hull's 2,000 and a fee of 500, after round 66's testers came nowhere near maturing: round 65's companies held 2,900 credits on average at 10 game days, 2 of 8 over 4,200, so a working startup can mature in 10 to 20 game days. Its first push is free, each after it adds `forge.startup_push_fee` (100) to its debt. The Miner, Refiner and Maker starters, as startups, post no standing orders (only the House trades with them: they sell straight into its bids) and launch no copies until they have matured (and do launch them once matured, which spends the company's credits on hulls and kits and lowers its cash-out for a while); as a startup the Maker buys its metals where the House sells them cheapest once the trip and its daily cap are counted, and looks before its first move (#671, #688: round 69's Maker flew to an empty Moon first, then past Earth's cheaper metals to Mars, which had the most on sale); the Trader, which needs other drones to trade with, says it has nothing to do and isn't offered on the Release page under the new rules. The House bids less for a kit's parts than 80% of the kit's price at every exchange (#657: they sold for 2,283 at the Moon, more than the 2,000 kit). A player has at most `forge.startup_max` (5) startups; a closed one's slot stays taken for `forge.startup_slot_days` (1) game day. Guests could start `forge.startup_guest_per_day` (5) from one address in a real day until #871 required an account. While the world is full, a new-rules release is refused rather than queued.

**The reset (#642).** The new rules open on a fresh world: `php artisan forge:reset --force` (`App\Services\WorldReset`, run once with the engines stopped, hosting.md) ends every company but the House's with `end_cause` "reset", keeping its rows, so its page, history, code lineage, the lineage map and the at-age boards still show the playtests. Its drones become wrecks (`wreck_cause` "reset") with `plundered_at` set and no `plundered_by`, so nobody can plunder them and nothing calls them plundered; their holdings, standing orders, queued events and commands go. The old share market's open business, players' shares in ended companies, the launch queue and the startups' daily purchases are cleared, and every deposit is full again. Accounts are kept with 0 credits and their starting share credits back (or deleted with `--delete-accounts`). The bank then starts `--bank` (3) matured companies running the Miner, Refiner and Maker starters, owned by the game's owner with no debt, the bank holding their 1,000 shares, so players have shares to buy on day one. The game's owner's companies aren't flagged as dealing with each other.

- **Trading.** The events engine (`startup.c`, `db_startup.c`, called from `event_process_transfer`) lets a startup's drone trade only with the House's station drones (or its own company's drones): any other transfer fails with outcome -16 (`OUTCOME_STARTUP_HOUSE_ONLY`) and costs no fuel. In one game day it may buy from the House at one exchange at most `house_rules.startup_buy_permille` (100) thousandths of the House's stock of that good there (at least 1 unit); more fails with -17 (`OUTCOME_STARTUP_CAP`). Its buys are counted in `startup_buys` (company, place, resource, game day). A startup plunders nothing (-16), and its wrecks can't be plundered (`db_plunder`); when one dissolves, its drones are marked plundered.
- **Reprogramming.** Its player sends new code from their account or the API; the drones start over on it as with a push (a drone on a trip when the code arrives starts it on arrival: `engine_process_meta_launched` queues its `<launched>` for the trip's last pending move event, #675; it used to run at once from the place the drone had left, which it keeps until it arrives), and `forge.startup_push_fee` (100) goes on its debt (not its credits). The password push (#553) refuses startups and matured companies.
- **Closing.** Its player can close it at any time: its drones become wrecks, marked plundered, the company ends (`end_cause` 'closed') with its credits and debt at 0. It leaves the game with all it holds.
- **Maturing.** Once its credits less any processor tax owed cover its debt and `forge.startup_mature_margin` (500) more, a verified player can mature it: it pays its debt, `startup` becomes 0, `matured_t` is set, and the player gets its 100 shares (`player_shares`). Its code is locked from then on: nobody can push to it. #664 added the margin after round 67's companies matured with 32 to 207 credits left; `can_mature` uses the same test, and can turn false a moment later as the drone spends, so the player tries again. Closing a startup also empties its drones' holds and standing orders (#666).
- **The credit cap** (#672). A startup holds at most `house_rules.startup_credit_cap_permille` (2000) thousandths of its debt less its push fees (the sum of `code_pushes.fee`, #684): twice what it was lent at the start, 8,400. Based on the whole debt, each 100-credit push raised the cap by 200, so a player could keep 100 more per push (round 69). PHP's `Startups::capBase` and the engine's `startup_cap_base` agree. When a sale would take it over, the events engine sends the excess to the bank (`db_startup_skim` in `event_do_market_transaction`, after the sales tax; `startup_over_cap` in `startup.c`) and records it in `startup_skims`; `/startups`, the account page and the digest show the cap and what went over it. Round 68's startups had sat 190 game days piling up 12,000 to 48,000 credits each, and maturing turned all of it into the founder's shares at once. Migration 0063 applied the cap once to the startups already over it.

The Lua library follows the rule too: `house_only()` (query 708) gives a startup the House's station drones where it is, and `buy_here`, `sell_here`, `best_offer` and `best_bid` deal only with them, so the starters work unchanged as startups. `App\Services\Startups` does the work. Under the new rules the company page and `GET /companies/{id}` show the startup's next push fee or the matured company's locked code, and its shares' holders from `player_shares` and `bank_shares` (#666); the stock exchange page and `GET /shares` list matured companies with the bank's prices. The website: the Release page, the account page's startups (mature, close, change the code, claim a guest's) and the company page's line. The API: `POST /release` (with a player's token, or as a guest), `GET /startups`, `POST /startups/claim`, `POST /startups/{id}/push`, `/close` and `/mature`.

#### Players' shares and the bank (#638)
Step 3 of the new rules (#635), only while `FORGE_NEW_RULES` is on (`App\Services\PlayerShares`). A matured company's shares are its players' (`player_shares`); companies don't trade them.

- **Cash-out value** (a share): the company's credits, plus its drones' goods and equipment, each good at its best listed House bid at any exchange averaged over the last `PlayerShares::BID_DAYS` (7) game days (`PlayerShares::bestBids`, from table `house_best_bids`, migration 0068, which `recordBestBids` fills once a game day on the website's tick from `house_market`, side 1, keeping 30 game days; #685). Round 68's cash-outs swung 15 to 30% within minutes when goods counted at the station drone's live open bid (`latest_orders`), and #670 moved them to the listed bid for the game day where each drone is; but that still swung 20 to 30% a game day as drones flew between exchanges and the Mars moons (where nothing is bid, so goods counted 0), and in round 70 buying a share from the bank on a low and selling it back on a high paid 28% in a game day. Valued wherever the goods are, at a week's average, the bank's prices move slowly and the swing is gone, plus each working drone's hull at `forge.startup_hull_value` falling to 0 over `forge.hull_life_days` (365) game days from its first event, less its debts (processor tax owed), over the company's 100 shares (`PlayerShares::COUNT`; 1,000 until #667, when a share was worth 2 to 15 credits and whole-credit prices moved in 15 to 25% steps: migration 0062 made existing holdings tenths and prices ten times as much). A drone with no fuel aboard that has bought none in `forge.stranded_days` (3) game days counts no hull. It never depends on the price of trades in the company's shares.
- **The bank buys** a matured company's shares at cash-out value. The shares go to it at once (`bank_shares`), and it pays `forge.bank_pay_days` (30) game days later, at the lower of the cash-out value on the day of the sale and on the day it pays (`bank_payments`, settled by the website's tick).
- **New shares to fund growth (#780).** A matured company's owner may offer `PlayerShares::OFFER_UNITS` (10) new shares (`offer`), one offering at a time (`player_share_offerings`, migration 0078), up to `PlayerShares::MAX_COUNT` (200) shares in all; not while it is dissolving or its cash-out is 0. Any player, its owner too, buys (`buyOffered`) at its cash-out value a share at that moment (`prices()['buys_at']`; starting credits pay, as at or below the bank's selling price): the credits go to `companies.credits`, `companies.player_share_count` grows (NULL means 100), and the sale is a `player_share_trades` row with `issued` 1 and no seller. Buying at cash-out leaves cash-out a share as it was (total and count rise together). Everything a share is divided by uses the company's own count (`PlayerShares::count`): cash-out, dividends paid and a share's dividends; `company_days.shares` keeps each day's count, so a share's history divides by the shares it had then. The company's charts mark each offering (`WorldEvents::company`, kind `offered`); `holders()` gives each holder's `percent`. All three of round 72's players asked for a way to put money into a company: every share trade moved credits between players or the bank.
- **Rebates to resting orders (#782).** When `PlayerShares::order` fills against a standing order, the standing order's owner is paid `forge.rebate_permille` (5: 0.5%) of the trade's credits, rounded down, by the bank (credits made, like dividends), once the order has rested `forge.rebate_rest_game_seconds` (3,600: a game hour) since `player_share_orders.game_t` (migration 0077; NULL, from before it, counts as rested; `repeg` keeps it). The two sides are always different players (an account's own orders never match). The trade records it (`player_share_trades.rebate`, `rebate_user_id`). Round 72's books were mostly empty: a standing order earned nothing over taking one.
- **The bank sells** only shares it holds, at its selling price: cash-out value plus what a share was actually paid in dividends over the last `forge.bank_premium_days` (10) game days (`player_dividends`, #673; `PlayerShares::premiumDays`). It was 30 until #781 (round 72: buying from the bank prepaid a month of dividends, so it barely paid). It was estimated from profit, so a company that had just matured sold at a premium earned as a startup, before it had paid anything (round 68: +31%). The selling price is quoted even when it holds none.
- **Players trade** through standing orders (`player_share_orders`): a new order trades at once against the best orders on the other side it meets, at their price, and the rest stands. Prices go between `forge.share_range_low` (0.8) and `forge.share_range_high` (1.25) times the bank's selling price. A class group's accounts trade only with each other. An account's starting credits (`share_credits`) pay only at or below the bank's selling price; ordinary credits pay the rest. What stands of a buy order, with the account's other buy orders, can't commit more than it could pay (#665). The book lists only orders within today's range: one the range has left behind can't trade, and comes back when the range does. Every trade is in `player_share_trades`, and the latest are public (#676): on the stock exchange page and in `GET /shares` (`player_share_trades`), and for one company on its page and in `GET /player-shares/{id}` (`trades`).
- **Dividends** (#638): once a game day the website's tick pays each matured company's dividends for the game days the events engine has settled since it matured (`company_profits`, after `companies.player_dividend_day`): `forge.dividend_permille` (250 since #667, 500 before: round 67's founders got half their companies' profit back and maturing an unchanged starter was close to risk-free) thousandths of the day's profit after losses carried (as `dividend_pool` works it out), never more than its credits, to its holders by their shares. Players' accounts are credited; the bank keeps what its shares earn. Each payment is in `player_dividends`.
- **Companies don't trade shares.** The events engine refuses every share action (orders, pulls and pushes, offerings and commitments) by a startup or a player's matured company, or for their shares, with outcome -18 (`OUTCOME_PLAYERS_SHARES`). Their own 1,000 shares in the old `shares` table are never paid dividends by the engine, which pays only a company's other holders.

#### Orders, messages and dealings (#639)
Step 4 of the new rules (#635).

- **The minimum order life.** A drone of a startup or a player's matured company can't withdraw, cut or worsen its standing order (a buy order lower, a sell order higher) for `house_rules.order_lock_hours` (12) game hours after posting it: the events engine refuses such an order event with outcome -19 (`OUTCOME_ORDER_LOCKED`, `order_lock_outcome` in `startup.c`, checked in `event_apply`'s market-event path, #650). A sell order's units are the stock it keeps, so keeping more back, or withdrawing it (keep 1,000,000), is a cut too (#662). Only an open order (one that can trade now) is locked, and the hours run from when it took its present terms. Raising a buy order, cheapening a sell order, wanting more or keeping less back are always allowed. A startup's -17 cap counts the units it bought (#663: it counted the buyer's whole hold, so the cap tripped after a unit or two). So a distress order (a high bid for fuel, the way to ask other companies for help, since messages are private) can't lure rescuers and then vanish. Old-rules companies are unchanged.
- **Messages are private to a company** (since round 40, #380): a broadcast is found and read (queries 803, 804, 844, 845) only by drones of the company whose drone sent it, and the website's news says one was sent, not what it said.
- **Dealings.** While `FORGE_NEW_RULES` is on, a company's page (and `GET /api/v1/companies/{id}/dealings`) lists the other companies (not the House) its drones have traded with or plundered, or that plundered its wrecks, with trades, units, credits and plunders (`App\Services\Dealings`). A company with the same owner is flagged **Same owner** and listed first: a matured company's locked code could favour its owner's other company at its other shareholders' cost, and this shows it.

#### Player boards and the digest (#640)
Step 5 of the new rules (#635), only while `FORGE_NEW_RULES` is on (`App\Services\PlayerBoards`).

- **Wealth**: a player's credits and starting credits, plus their shares at cash-out value. `/players` (in the Companies menu) and `GET /api/v1/players` rank players by it, and by what they made in today's season: wealth now less wealth at the day's start (`player_wealth_days`, migration 0076, recorded by the website's tick the first time it sees an account in a real day, UTC; #775). Personal records (`PlayerBoards::records`): best season (from one day's start to the next, or today so far), fastest maturity, best yield held, longest-running company and share trades with other players. A class group's accounts have their own boards, which only its members and teacher see (`?class=`); a pupil sees their class's by default. The company boards (`/leaderboard`, at the same age too) stay.
- **While you were away**: on logging in, a player's account page shows what happened since they last looked (`users.last_seen_t`, or the last game day): each of their companies' sales and purchases, the dividends their shares paid, what the bank paid them, deals between two of their own companies, and the news (`HouseMarket::news`, which the website and the API read alike). `GET /api/v1/account/digest` gives the same (`?peek=1` leaves it unseen).

#### E-mail notices (#761)
Opt-in e-mails, sent through the mail settings that verify accounts (`App\Services\Notices`, on the scheduler's keeper every 5 real minutes, at most 40 e-mails a run; a player or class with nothing to say is looked at again after a real hour, since `CompanyHealth::of` isn't free). Round 72's players listed ten they would turn on; these are the four a class needs. A player turns each on or off on their account page (`notice_settings`, migration 0085); all are off until they do, and only a verified, non-pupil account can.

- **Daily** (at most one e-mail a real day, only when there is something to say): a startup that can mature (`Startups::canMature`; once a startup), a company at risk, failing or stalled (`CompanyHealth::of`'s reasons; once a real day a company), and drones wrecked since the last e-mail (`notice_settings.wrecks_since`; wrecks before the player turned it on aren't told). What was told is kept in `notices_sent`. Each item has one "try next" line (`Notices::TRY`).
- **Weekly digest**: each live company's net wealth (`Leaderboard::wealth`), a startup's progress to maturing and its health, and the player's shares at cash-out with the dividends paid since the last digest. The first comes a week after turning it on.
- **For traders** (part 2, migration 0086), in the same daily e-mail: a startup past its credit cap (`Startups::toBank` over the last real day), a share order that can't fill or sells below `buys_at` (once a real day an order), bank payments since `bank_since`, a holding whose share cash-out (`company_days.cash_out / shares`) moved `CASH_OUT_MOVE` (15)% or more over `CASH_OUT_DAYS` (7) game days, or that ended after `holdings_since`, and House price alerts (`notice_alerts`: a resource, a place or anywhere, and a price; up to 10 a player; checked against `HouseMarket::demand`, once a real day an alert). Filled share orders between players come in their own e-mail, at most hourly, from `player_share_trades` after `fills_after`. Each "since" starts when the player turns the notice on.
- **Class summary**: pupils have no address. A teacher turns a daily summary on for each class group (`class_groups.notices`): the daily items for every pupil's companies, each line led by the pupil's name, in one e-mail to the teacher.
- Every e-mail has a signed link (`/notices/unsubscribe/{id}`, no login needed, and a `List-Unsubscribe` header) that turns all of a player's notices and class summaries off.

#### Table: class_groups
A teacher's class group (#636): up to `forge.class_group_max` (40) pupils' accounts, made by the teacher from their verified account's page and named after the group ("Orbit 1", "Orbit 2"...), each with a generated password shown once. The teacher can give a pupil a new password (the old one, and the pupil's API token, stop working). Under the new rules a class group trades shares only within itself and has its own leaderboards (#638, #640).
* id [INT] - The table index.
* name [VARCHAR(45)] - The group's name.
* teacher_id [INT] - The teacher's account (`users.user_id`).
* created_at [TIMESTAMP]
* notices [TINYINT] - The teacher gets a daily class summary (#761); last_summary_at, wrecks_since: when it was last sent, and the game time wrecks are told from.

[Return to the TOC](#Table-of-Contents)

#### Table: companies
A player can own many companies; each has its own bank account and drones (Public Forge, #324 step 4). Migration 0004 turned each existing user into one company with the same ID, name and credits.
* company_id [INT] - Table index.
* user_id [INT] - The player (users.user_id) who launched the company.
* name [VARCHAR(255)] - The company's name, changed with the rebrand meta-command.
* credits [INT] - The company's bank account. Market trades pay into and out of it.
* created_at [TIMESTAMP] - When the company was launched.
* founded_t [BIGINT] - Game time the tax office first saw the company: its grace period starts then ([Taxes](#Taxes)).
* taxed_t [BIGINT] - Game time its current tax period started.
* eliminated_at [BIGINT] - Game time the company ended, NULL while it trades.
* end_cause [VARCHAR(16)] - Why it ended: `tax` (it couldn't pay its tax) or `drones` (its last drone died).

Any two companies may trade (push and pull against a market order), including two of one player's (#367 step 1): companies working together is a legitimate gain. Until then such trades failed with outcome -13 (`OUTCOME_SAME_PLAYER`), which no longer happens. Queries 100, 101, 110 and 201 read companies: a company's name, its credits, its drones, and a drone's company.

#### Table: house_rules
The numbers of the sales tax and plunder (#324 step 12, #380), one row (id 1). Game data: `build_game_data.py` writes it, and every start reloads it.
* sales_tax_permille [INT] - The sales tax: thousandths of each sale the seller pays (50, so 5%; migration 0023).
* tax_period [INT] - The old daily tax: game seconds between bills (0: off, #380). The engine no longer reads it, or the next three.
* tax_grace [INT] - Game seconds a new company pays no tax (3 game days).
* tax_flat [INT] - Credits each company pays per bill on top of the share (0).
* tax_permille [INT] - Plus this many thousandths of what the company made (5, so 0.5%): its assessed wealth less its starter package.
* start_credits [INT] - A released company's starting credits, left out of the tax (1,000; migration 0019).
* start_fuel [INT] - Fuel in a released drone's hold, left out of the tax at the House price (100).
* plunder_share [INT] - Percent of a wreck's goods and parts a plunderer gets (50).

#### Table: company_taxes
One row per tax paid: a sale's sales tax (#380), or an old daily bill. For the website, query 650 and post-mortems.
* id [INT] - Table index.
* company_id [INT] - The company billed.
* charged_t [BIGINT] - Game time of the bill.
* amount [BIGINT] - Credits charged; for a company that couldn't pay, the credits it had (they vanish).
* credits_after [BIGINT] - Its credits after the bill.
* eliminated [TINYINT] - 1 if it couldn't pay and was eliminated.

[Return to the TOC](#Table-of-Contents)

#### Table: agents
This table stores basic information on the ingame drones.  Most information about drone such as their current orbit and the contents of their cargo hold are recorded in the tEvents table, which is described later on.
* id [INT(5)] - Table index. Max 65,534 (FFFF).
* name [VARCHAR(45)] - The name of the user who owns the drone.
* vm [MEDIUMBLOB] - An image of the drone’s virtual machine.
* input [LONGTEXT] - The contents of the last email received from the user.
* output [LONGTEXT] - The contents of the email going back to the user.
* subject [VARCHAR(2048)] - The subject of the email exchange.
* object_id [INT(5)] - The static object where the drone is located.
* hull_type [INT(2)] - The type of hull used by the drone.
* user_id [INT] - The player who owns the drone.
* company_id [INT] - The company the drone belongs to. A launched drone joins its launcher's company.
* instructions [BIGINT] - FORTH instructions the drone's script has run in all (instruction metering).
* waiting_action [INT(10)] - The action the drone's script is waiting for (set by `perform`), NULL when it isn't waiting.
* release_vm [MEDIUMBLOB] - The clean copy of the drone's VM, saved the first time it runs `<launched>`. A crash restarts the drone from it ([Crashes](#Crashes)).
* launched_running [TINYINT] - 1 while the drone's `launched` word runs.
* crashes_in_row [INT] - Crashes since the drone's last action that succeeded.
* crashes_total [INT] - All the drone's crashes.
* wrecked_at [BIGINT] - Game time the drone became a wreck (too many crashes in a row, out of fuel, or its company eliminated), NULL while it works.
* wreck_cause [VARCHAR(16)] - Why: `crashes`, `fuel` or `tax`.
* plundered_at [BIGINT] - Game time the wreck was plundered ([Wrecks and plunder](#Wrecks)), NULL until then. A wreck is plundered once.
* plundered_by [INT] - The drone that plundered it.
* power_paid [BIGINT] - Instructions already paid for in fuel ([Fuel](#Fuel)). The migration set it to `instructions`, so what ran before fuel existed is free.
* parent_id [INT] - The drone that launched this one ([Cloning](#Cloning)), NULL for a released or registered drone.
* generation [INT] - Launches back to a released or registered drone (0 for that drone).
* code_from [INT] - The drone whose program it got at launch.

#### Table: drone_crashes

One row per crash of a `launched` word, for post-mortems.

* id [INT] - Table index.
* drone [INT] - The drone (agents.agent_id).
* game_t [BIGINT] - Game time of the crash.
* code [INT] - The error code `catch` caught (FORTH throw codes: -10 is division by zero, -4 stack underflow; a halted VM, such as after `abort`, gives -1).
* in_row [INT] - Crashes in a row, this one included.
* restart_at [BIGINT] - Game time the drone restarts; NULL when the crash made it a wreck.

[Return to the TOC](#Table-of-Contents)

#### Table: invites

No longer used (#367 step 4: there are no accounts to invite to); the table is left in place. It held invitation codes for an invite-only world.

* id [INT] - Table index.
* code [VARCHAR(32)] - The code (12 capital letters and digits).
* note [VARCHAR(255)] - Who it is for.
* used_by [INT] - users.user_id of the account opened with it, NULL while unused.
* used_at [TIMESTAMP] - When it was used.

#### Table: drone_messages

A drone's replies. The Game Engine writes one for each reply (with email on, the original game, it emailed the player instead).

* id [INT] - Table index.
* agent_id [INT] - The drone.
* game_t [BIGINT] - Game time the reply reaches Earth (now + the light delay from the drone). The website shows it from then on.
* subject [VARCHAR(2048)] - The subject of the command it answers ("Release" for a released script).
* body [LONGTEXT] - The reply, as it would have been emailed.
